ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u14	The next item is the debate on draft law Nos. 2081, 14, 197, 239, 314, 909, 1211, 1231, 1316, 1360, 1745, 1763, 2069 and 2084. I call the President of the Second Permanent Commission, Senator Palma, to report on the Commission's work.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u15	Madam President, the examination of the draft law on civil unions, which altogether engaged the Commission in some 70 sessions, including the round of hearings held in the Bureau, began on 18 June 2013, i.e. just under a month after the Commission was set up and, initially, Senators Cirinna and Falanga were co-rapporteurs of the measure. This examination concerned the draft law no. 15, 197, 204, 239, 314 and 393. In the session of 2 July 2013 the examination of the draft law no. 197, 239 and 314 were separated from the examination of the other draft laws. When examining these draft laws (Nos. 197, 239 and 314), in subsequent sessions, held between December 2013 and April 2014, was then jointly examined the draft law n. 14, for which the 4a On 24 July 2013, the Commission issued a favourable opinion with a recommendation, and no. 909, 1211, 1231 and 1360. Senator Cirinna, who in the meantime became the only rapporteur, at the outcome of the majority change, on 8 April 2014, presented to the Commission two sets of unified texts: the first for draft law no. 14, 197, 239, 314 and 909, on the subject of the discipline of cohabitation pacts and de facto cohabitations. The second, for the draft law no. 1211, 1231 and 1360 on the regulation of civil unions between persons of the same sex. On 24 June 2014, the examination of a further draft law, No. 1316, was carried out. On 2 July 2014, the rapporteur proposed the correct, unified text scheme, which brought together the themes covered by the two previous schemes, was published. A wide range of hearings took place in the Bureau from 13 January to 19 February 2015. On 24 February 2015, the examination of the drafts of law was resumed with the conjunction of the examination of two further drafts of law, No. 1745 and No. 1763. Subsequently, at its meeting on 17 March 2015, a new unified text was presented by the rapporteur, following the presentation of an alternative unified text, proposed by Senators Caliendo, Falanga, Malan and Cardiello, at its meeting on 25 March 2015. On 26 March, the Commission adopted as its basic text the text which was last submitted by the rapporteur and set the deadline for tabling amendments on 7 May 2015. On 15 April 2015, the unified text received a non-obstacle opinion with comments from 14a Commission and on 12 May 2015 a favourable opinion with comments from the 1st Commission. As of 9 June 2015, an illustration of the first block of amendments tabled, some 3,600, 800 of which were withdrawn before the beginning of the examination, began. Of the remaining 2,800, the Presidency of the Commission has declared the impossibility or inadmissibility of 1,461 amendments. Following the rapporteur's submission of three new amendments, 279 sub-binding proposals were tabled and of these 110 were declared inadmissible or inadmissible by the Presidency of the Commission. On 7 and 14 July, the first Commission opinion on the amendments was received: partly contrary to, partly non-obstacle with conditions, partly non-obstacle with observations, partly non-obstacle, while it was awaiting the opinion of the Budget Commission on the unified text and amendments, having the Budget Commission requested the Government to submit a technical report in order to assess the financial coverage of certain forecasts contained in the unified text. Needless to say, the Commission could not proceed to the vote on the amendments in the absence of the opinion of the Budget Commission and without obtaining the technical report of the Government for this purpose submitted by the Budget Commission itself. On 29 July 2015, the above-mentioned opinion of the Budget Commission was therefore received: non-obstacle, conditional, in accordance with Article 81 of the Constitution, on the text, and non-obstacle and partly contrary, in accordance with Article 81 of the Constitution, on the amendments, followed by the submission of a new amendment by the rapporteur with financial cover and three new sub-emerging proposals. Once the new opinion of the Budget Committee had been obtained, the vote on the amendments was moved to the stage, which began as soon as the summer recess of the Senate's work was completed, which saw the Justice Commission engaged for thirteen sessions, at which 318 amendments were examined. Of these, two were adopted, 74 were rejected, while the remaining were withdrawn or declared to be dead or foreclosed, which is to say that a total of 318 amendments were adopted at the thirteen sittings. A further 578 amendments were withdrawn on 8 and 9 October. It should be recalled, with reference to the number of sittings (Thirteen, beginning early September this year), that the Commission's work has been seriously stalled as a result of the timetable for constitutional reforms in this House, because, due to the formation of that calendar and often the simultaneous meeting of the parliamentary groups with regard to constitutional reform, the meetings which had already been held have been disconcerted. Finally, during the night session on 12 October, the examination of three other draft laws was initiated (the draft law nn. 2069, 2081 and 2084) which have been combined with the draft laws already mentioned in the examination. It should be noted that, at today's date, if we were still in the Commission, 518 amendments would remain to be voted on. I believe that my report, in keeping exclusively with the progress of the work, must necessarily stop here and cannot in any way concern the measure which the Conference of Leaders has indicated as a basic text. However, as a result of some of the speeches that took place yesterday, I would like to say that I find the criticism that some people have made to the Commission that they have not committed themselves to such a measure absolutely ingenious. This very delicate measure has been under discussion for two years. All the draft laws that have been presented have been brought together and related, and the issues have been dealt with at the stage of the general discussion. Of course, I have to say that if there had not been an exasperated deepening of all the amendments in the Commission, there would probably have been more room for discussion and a more serene debate.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u32	BONFRISCO (CoR). Madam President, we are examining and voting on a measure which intends to intervene (even if late) on legislative decree 58 of 1998, the so-called TUF (single text of finance) and, in particular, on the activity of personalised assistance carried out by intermediaries, mediated a wide range of services, often complex, in favour of those who want to invest a share of their resources in the financial market. This is an activity which, starting from 2007, is defined as investment advice by the MiFID Directive, which does not constitute a new type of activity: it was already provided for by Law No 1 of 1991, which included it among the securities intermediation activities (what we now call investment services) reserved for banks and so-called SIMs; it constitutes, however, an innovation in the relationship between intermediary and investor. The globalisation of financial markets and the increasing variety and complexity of products continue to make investors' choices dependent on intermediaries' suggestions. But the former can no longer be the instrument (often unconscious) in the hands of the latter, due to the obvious contiguity between advisory and management services. It is necessary, in our opinion, to raise the level of protection for the saver, his awareness of the risks (many times hidden) he meets when they face an investment. It is therefore necessary to increase the transparency and clarity of the activity of the various intermediaries, also with respect to the role played, which has a significant impact on the allocation of savings: one thing is the promoters, another is the consultants and, among them, those who provide advice instrumental to the sale of certain financial products and those who, instead, provide independent advice (i.e. in the exclusive interest of the customer and paid by him) both as a natural person and as a company. It is a significant novelty, which we consider of particular importance in our financial landscape, which became operational with great effort and after several attempts only in 2009, with the entry into force of Articles 18-a and 18-ter of the single text of finance; instead, the planned establishment of a special register of natural persons, within which to form a specific section for companies, held by a special body, has remained on paper. Since 2009, the regulatory path has been marked by the alternation of provisions and derogations, the only result of which has been to bring the register to a dead track due to the lack of constitution of the organism. To this, however, it intends to refer to the measure that we are discussing with the amendments made by Article 58 of the TUF. It proposes a kind of reorganisation of the system, based essentially on a single register of financial advisors, in which the consultants (natural and legal persons) join together, as well as the current financial promoters, who assume the diction of financial advisors qualified for the off-site offer, on the basis of the consideration, expressed in the introductory report, for which the current definition of financial promoter, linked to promotion and placement, is exceeded by market practice which, using the off-site offer as one of the means of providing the advisory service, ensures the application of the legislation on adequacy to protect the saver. The maintenance of this register is entrusted to a new body, of associative type, with legal personality, supervised by CONSOB, to define and verify the implementation of the principles according to which the body must operate. Of course, it would have been better to make the system defined by Articles 18-a and 18-b of the TUF operational, which would certainly have made more clarity than the three different figures of intermediaries. Perhaps, just to the customers less able to correctly select the possible risks may not be completely clear the difference between the new figure of promoter and that of the consultant! At this point, however, the thrust of the Commission's dismissal is well, a solution which I believe is acceptable, if only because it fills the gap that has been created and which for far too long has remained in a situation of financial crisis as worrying as ever. However, ladies and gentlemen, it seems to me more interesting than the content of this measure. Presented in July last year, it was examined in November when the deadline for tabling amendments was set and then postponed. In January of this year the rapporteur presented an amendment on which the Government expressed a favourable opinion and, after some referrals, rewritten a couple of sub-amendments submitted by the oppositions, on which the Government expressed its favourable opinion. So we arrive at 5 August when the Commission dismisses the text to the attention of this House. That text, however, is nothing more than the re-imposition of an amendment adopted at the last budgetary session, which has found no room within the now traditional maxi amendment approving the Stability Act. Ten months have passed to build a text on which there had already been a positive assessment, the same one that seems to be expressed today by political forces, since no amendments have been tabled. Today, therefore, the Senate should deliver to the House a text on which the other branch of Parliament could agree in a short time. But will this text ever become law, Mr. Undersecretary, or will we have to wait any longer? It would seem trivial questions, however, doubt seems legitimate, given the time we have taken. I recall, in fact, that the law n. 114 of 2015, the law of European delegation 2014, approved by the Chamber on 2 July last, in Article 9 contains, among others, a delegation to the Government (the one for the implementation of Directive n. 65 of 2014, on markets in financial instruments) concerning financial advisors and financial promoters, which in essence should be brought together in a single register, held by an associative body and with legal personality. So the question is: what will happen after the Senate vote? Will the measure continue its process in the House or will it stop because the Government will want to implement the delegation? And in this case how long will it take? All this takes place in front of MiFID II which, since January 2017, should revolutionise the sector, with the stated objective of strengthening the protection of investors "per minute" with specific measures on financial products, also providing for a greater specification of the independent advisory service and strengthening the obligations of communication to customers. Will the famous phrase that "Everything is late in Italy, when it comes to starting a job, or everything is early when it comes to stopping it" be right? I would remind you that, in addition to the technical assessments and the excellent work of the rapporteur and the entire Commission chaired by Senator Marino, this text comes to fruition at a time when the strong transformation and restructuring that the banking sector will be forced to do, including the latest innovations in European surveillance, allow the horizon to be opened to a very large number of personalities and professionalism that could emerge from the banking world and, in this way, be able to find, thanks to the work carried out by the Senate, as quickly as possible other placements. New markets are opening up, which we must encourage, including for the relocation of these people. That is why our vote will certainly be in favour. [[Applause from Senator Bruni.]]

The body for the management of the lists of financial agents and credit brokers, the setting up of a single register, has three separate sections that the rapporteur, Senator Ricchiuti, whom I thank, has well understood and explained to the House with regard to the type of activity carried out by the parties, who assume the following names: the financial advisors qualified for the off-site offer (the current financial promoters), the independent natural financial advisors (the current financial advisors) and the financial advisors. ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u44	BOTTICS (M5S). Mr President, the Senate Act 1559 on the reorganization of financial advice is a measure which was born with the best intentions and has had a very long gestation in this House. The good intentions, as stated in the explanatory report, were to rationalise the system of supervision of financial promoters and financial advisors through amendments to the single text of the provisions on financial intermediation, legislative decree of 24 February 1998 n. 58. It is true that it is planned to set up a single register managed by a single body in this field which is similar to the model laid down in other areas of our legal system. However, it is also foreseen to rename the individuals who will be included in the single register. Those that are currently the financial promoters will be defined as financial advisors qualified for the off-site offer. At first glance it may seem a merely formal or lexical issue, but it is enough to explain briefly the difference between promoters and financial advisors to prove that this is not the case. In fact, while a financial promoter is a person acting under the mandate of a financial intermediary - such as, for example, a bank - trying to sell certain financial products - in short, he is a bit like a seller - the financial consultant, on the other hand, is a person who cannot be employed by a company, but is remunerated by its customers in exchange for the investment advice he provides. [[Brusio]] Colleagues, if possible, I ask you to lower your voice.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u45	Colleagues, if we all lower the tone of our voices, we can speak and understand.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u46	BOTTICS (M5S). In short, the financial advisor is an independent professional. At this point it should be clear why the new names introduced by this bill are not merely nominal changes, but something much more dangerous. In fact, they can easily cause confusion in the saving client, causing him to feel that he is facing an independent professional rather than a person acting on a specific mandate of a certain financial intermediary. And I would like to remind you that some lawsuits stem precisely from the fact that the financial promoter had contracts signed outside his home, while he was supposed to abide by certain rules. With this new rule you will be able to do so and, therefore, the saver customer will be even more deceived. In the draft law under consideration, however, some... [[Brusio. ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u48	BOTTICS (M5S). I see how much interest there is in this matter, since we took charge of it a year ago. It was a long delivery, let's put it this way. The sanctioning system has been increased for transparency, but it is always little. It should also be stressed that the regulation to regulate the functioning of the supervisory body and to keep the single register will be adopted at CONSOB's sole discretion. It would have been more appropriate for this regulation to be adopted, perhaps always on a proposal from CONSOB, but according to the provisions of Article 17 (2) of Law No 400 of 1998, which provides that the regulations are adopted by decree of the President of the Republic, after resolution of the Council of Ministers; then the Council of State may also be heard, after the opinion of the relevant parliamentary committees. Another problem in this bill is the amendment to paragraph 8 (b), which provides for the replacement of conciliation procedures provided for in Legislative Decree No 179 of 2007 by the procedure for the out-of-court settlement of disputes governed by CONSOB. Although it is true that it is the conciliation provided for in Legislative Decree No 179 of 2007 that the procedure of out-of-court resolution of CONSOB are both alternative methods of dispute resolution, the so-called ADRs (Alternative dispute resolution), it should be pointed out that, while the first is governed by a law, the second is entirely referred to the regulatory action of CONSOB. Therefore, although at first instance, the protection of the saver is restored to the regulatory action and discretion of CONSOB. Senator Ricchiuti recalled, presenting an agenda, that paragraph 1-a of Article 5 of Legislative Decree n. 28 of 2010 is a transitional rule with effect for four years: after the first two years, a monitoring of the results of such experimentation, i.e. mediations, should have been carried out, which never appears to have been done, but the transition to mediation is made mandatory, without knowing if this institution works. ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u49	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u51	SCIASCIA (FI-PdL XVII). Madam President, the measure under consideration, promoted in 2014 by several political parties and substantially amended by the Senate Finance Commission, aims to supplement the provisions of Part Two of Legislative Decree No 58 of 1998, i.e. the single text of the provisions on financial intermediation. I remember that the financial intermediation sector has had exponential growth over the last decade and is currently employing a few thousand operators. Hence the need to renew the obsolete legislation that regulates the sector not only to regulate the activity of operators, but also to give reliable references to customers. The measure has already been extensively explained by the rapporteur. In a nutshell, it amends the former supervisory body, the sole register of financial promoters, which is transformed into the new single register of financial advisors, whose members - I recall - are essentially of elective origin of consultants from their professional associations. And it's under the control of CONSOB. Specific rules are laid down for the registration and application of penalties for irregularities committed by members, with the institution of an out-of-court dispute settlement procedure with customers, which is very important and innovative. A government concession fee of EUR 168 has also been introduced for new entries, since the members of the old register will automatically pass into the new institution without charge. I should like to conclude that this is a long-awaited measure for the entire intermediary sector. Therefore, the Forza Italia Group votes in favour. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u52	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u54	MARINO Mauro Maria (PD). Madam President, Mr President-in-Office of the Government, ladies and gentlemen, the draft law for which I am voting in favour on behalf of the Group of the Democratic Party is the result of a parliamentary initiative which has collected, since the beginning of the legislature, the demands of those who see the regulation and rationalization of the register of persons entitled to propose financial instruments as a useful tool in order to increase the transparency of financial operators and, by this way, to better protect savers. The lesson of the financial crisis, which on other fronts of greater scope and scope has induced and imposed a wide revision of the banking and financial rules, has suggested to intervene in a specific and limited segment of the financial activity, but that it has significant reflections in the relationship between the saver and qualified subjects, today defined "financial promoters" and tomorrow in the new denomination "consultants entitled to the offer out of office." Mrs Ricchiuti's report and Senator Rossi's general intervention have focused on the technical specifications of the proposed standards and on the rapporteur, as well as on the Commission as a whole, because it was objectively a choral work, give her appreciation for the work she has done. I would like to reiterate on this occasion a concept that I have already expressed last in the examination of the draft law on the delegation of trusts and, before that, on the law of tax delegation. Parliament's legislative initiative is - I would say again - an important and important channel for the work of each Member of Parliament, beyond the obvious and undisputed primacy of the Executive in the implementation of the Government's direction. I would like to emphasise this more than ever also in the light of the statements made by some of my colleagues now. Perhaps, in seeing the glass half full and not half empty, in the will and determination of the Commission and Members of Parliament I still find the element of affirmation - which has also happened for other measures - of Parliament's will to assert its primacy in the legislative field. In the specific context of the measure under consideration, it was necessary to translate into a draft law the themes and solutions which had been developed in a different context such as that of the iter of a decree-law, whose rigid links, in terms of timing and consistency of content, have not allowed the adoption of amendments of similar content to that of the draft law. Once the process had started, it was, moreover, given the opportunity for a positive examination in the Commission of an amendment in the examination of last year's draft stability law (this is the reference made by Senator Bonfrisco). Again, an assessment which is not appropriate for legislative intervention has hindered its adoption. In summary, the legislative solution had also been identified and agreed with the most directly involved parties and various procedural instruments had been used (even without a result), despite the unanimous agreement to achieve an objective that was considered relevant and to which the previous speakers have already referred. I acknowledge that the Government has followed up on the commitments made several times in the Commission to enable the process to be concluded. But it is clear to me that there is a case of parliamentary stubbornness and stubbornness - I say this in positive terms - to which all the groups have contributed. Moreover, the failure to table amendments to the House makes it clear that we share the method, on the one hand, and on the substance, on the other. It must be taken into account - it has remembered, perhaps with a hint of concern, Senator Bonfrisco - that the law 9 July 2015, n. 114 (the law of European delegation with principles and guiding criteria for the implementation of the communitarian directive n. 65 of 2014 on the markets of the financial instruments), to article 9, letters a) and u), brings provisions in the matter of revision of the register of the promoters and the financial advisors. This is a further way to intervene with law on this matter and I hope that the time for consideration of the House of Members will be absolutely fast - in this sense I said before that I want to see the glass half full - precisely to affirm Parliament's legislative primacy. And I hope that, once the process in the Senate has been completed, the time for examination in the other branch of Parliament will enable the draft law to be adopted quickly and definitively. He wondered whether this was a request from the Senate Commission or a personal wish. My statement is to be a simple response to what was said on 9 July last by the President of the Financial Promoters' Register, on which it is worth reflecting. She said: We hope that by the end of the year the legislator will be able to conclude the process envisaged for the approval of these rules so necessary to achieve the full protection of the saver and its propulsive participation in the real economy and entrepreneurship in particular (...). Further procrastination of the transitional situation that the sector has undergone for eight years would reveal a failure of our system, which is not compatible with the Community regulatory dynamics for the protection of investors and the market. What better way than these words gives a sense of the need to intervene with reform in this area? These words were spoken by the person himself who was in a state of reform. I am convinced that the division of roles between legislative and executive still has its own theoretical and intellectual legitimacy, as well as politics by which I do not tell the Government to abdicate or weaken its role of primary direction, but I stress that the work in the Commission on specific areas of technical content remains an essential requirement of legislative work. If the government does the government - as I like to repeat and often hear the commissioners say - and Parliament does the Parliament, it benefits the institutional system as a whole and the legislative product improves, finding itself more in line with the expectations of the stakeholders. I believe that this measure adds a further stage to a path that has started very far away. It began in 1985 with the regulation of home selling of real estate values, and then developed with the so-called SIM Act of 1991, the Eurosim Decree of 1996 and the MiFID Directives of 2004 and MiFID II of 2014, of which the implementing rule is now being defined which - as Senator Bonfrisco recalls - will enter into force in 2017. I like to remember all this not only for historical memory, but also to demonstrate that, slowly, in this period, it has confirmed itself and imposed itself - and, therefore, in this I also feel to respond to the notations of Senator Bottici - the passage of the networks from the solicitation activity to home of the public saving to an effective activity of consulting to the investor, from a logic centered on the specific product marketed to a centered on the total service rendered to the customer. It is a virtuous model, which builds its strength on customer satisfaction and marries with an increasingly targeted regulation for investor protection. This is the theme that has moved the Commission's action as a whole, regardless of the individual political parties. And this has been more necessary than ever, since since 2007 - I stress since 2007 - there has been a continuing tolerance for the transitional provision of the advisory service to savers by persons who have been deprived of any form of public control, solely because of the inability to set up an autonomous body for the category. As we begin to conclude, beyond this specific intervention, we believe that it is necessary to embrace this issue with a general vision, with regard to the vocational and ethical training of financial advisors, to marry with the professional and cultural growth of the financial consultant. The financial promoter is now aware of being the main channel for the transmission of financial information to customers. The role of promoters has therefore evolved in favour of financial advice and there is already only in this response to Senator Bottici's concerns. And, above all, this comes from the respect of the strict expectations of compliance to guarantee a always fully informed and aware saver of the characteristics of the financial products offered. All this, however, is not enough, because one must necessarily accompany the financial education of the client. In conclusion, I would like to mention here the draft law which was incardinated in the 7th Commission, which concerns financial education. The education of savers is one of the key elements in the investor protection strategy and is essential to increase the effectiveness of supervisory action. A public financial education policy helps to make savers aware of their investment choices. It is, therefore, two sides of the same coin and to have an overall vision. Today the savers are more informed and aware of their investments and we hope they are even more confident: it is, in fact, a mass of almost 4,000 billion euros. In savers, however, the awareness of the role of the consultant and the importance of his assistance activity has also been consolidated, with a view to an optimal allocation of their savings. With the approval of the measure under consideration, Parliament not only carries out a small reform but also makes a significant contribution to the sector, but it also contributes to a process of financial maturity which - I am sure - will be reflected positively in the country, finally completing a process that has been waiting to be carried out for eight years. Madam President, you have asked me to explain my request. Therefore, I would like to ask your attention, since it is above all to you that I am addressing! PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. You're right, Senator Endrizzi, they were handing me the list of members to speak in the debate. I apologize. ENDRIZZI (M5S). As I said, today the abolition of gambling advertising has a single purpose, the protection of health, and we want to make it subject to the approval of a rule, such as the bill signed by Senator Mirabelli, which instead has many other issues at stake, often in conflict with health, for example, the maintenance of tax revenues and interventions on anti-mafia legislation, authorisations, and the powers of the quaestor and so on. We want there to be no barter between the health of the citizens and the other issues that are necessary. The government has dropped the tax delegation, we are proposing a measure that has an impact on health especially of young people and you believe that these two things are similar, as was the same issue and I say that this is the premise of a barter: the health of young people in exchange for favours of lobbying. This is the intent that you have consumed and that you are allowing! [[Applause from the M5S Group]] I do not know if there have been agreements between you before, but certainly the rumor, the chatter that followed the announcement in the Chamber of our request yesterday, by President Grasso, makes me think that some pebbles in the wasp have arrived and these are the bites that come today, but not to us, to a generation of boys. 51 percent of minors have already started gambling: 4 percent say they did because they saw advertising, but another 7 percent say they did because they thought winning was easy and who told them that winning is easy if not TV commercials? [[Applause from the M5S Group]] Although it is forbidden for minors, among young people we have about 9 percent of problematic players in the North, 10 percent in the Center and more than 12 percent in the South; what do we want to wait for? The comforts of Secretary Baretta, who has to agree with Lottomatica? Because that is what happened: the tax delegation received it first from Parliament's lobby, which was the holder of that delegation! This, Madam President, was an intervention on the order of business. Now I begin to explain the measure on the substance and hope to have the time; at least he will give me this. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Definitely, Senator. ENDRIZZI (M5S). Madam President, in Italian society, gambling has always been considered to be at risk and was not by chance prohibited by law on the whole of Italy, except for a derogation. The concessions were really limited at one time. For low latency games, that is, with a short time between the bet and the outcome, such as roulette and slot machines, practice was allowed in particular and limited places, such as casinos and racetracks. Since the mid-nineties, however, concessions have exploded and have brought highly impacting health games, the most dangerous in addiction, to be available and accessible everywhere. Citizens of the municipality of Campione d'Italia cannot play in their casino and it is a protective measure, and we must act today to curb the new demand. Marketing has become asphyxiating and has trodden the hand by exploiting the crisis in an ignoble way and promising the solution of problems, from home to income, through competitions, with misleading advertisements on the real chance of winning and this, Madam President, impacts especially on young people. In fact, they see narrowing the opportunities of a life project, because due to the precariousness and lack of training they are condemned to an instrument of competition with China instead of having opportunities for training and entrepreneurship that instead put Italy in competition with Germany. This distrust pours into a propensity to play that is induced very quickly through messages that have now entered colonizing the youth imagination. We have 15 million regular players in Italy. Gambling exploded, multiplying several times during the crisis, with a 30 percent rise in 2011. Concerns have already been expressed by the Anti-drug Policy Department of the Presidency of the Council of Ministers in a report that evoked a real social emergency. Italy is the first market in Europe for videolotteries that are considered the cocaine of gambling - a game lasts four seconds - and that lead a person to remain caught and enter a mechanism of compulsion in pressing the buttons. Sports betting is a growing industry because you can play in real time even during matches. Around the games - before, during and after - a bombing of gambling commercials takes place. They are the most watched broadcasts by young people and sports betting is the game in wider growth among young people and also among the very young. As I said, 51 percent of young people have already started playing, 32 percent hide the real lost sums from their parents and 5 percent of the daring males every day; one fifth of them devote themselves to five and more games at the same time. This year's Nomisma research has highlighted this data. Let me give you an example to understand the impact of the game on the thinking, on the psyche of young people. The following question had been asked to some youngsters: if someone gave you 100 euros, how many would you use for gambling? Almost half of those questioned replied that they would allocate a share of that amount to gamble. Of the 100 euro data, the average amount allocated for this purpose was 16%. I'm talking about guys who find themselves 100 euros unexpected would follow a dream in the drawer, something they care about and have been waiting for a long time. Then we have the paradox that those same young people, when they get married, ask for scratch cards and wins as a wedding gift. Do you understand the tragedy? We are faced with a colossal pedagogical emergency. It is not just recruitment of new players that is determined by advertising. It is also the promotion of a cultural model of unraveling, the search for an individual and selfish economic success that cries out with the principles of economic and social solidarity set out in Articles 2 and 4 of the Constitution. It must be considered, then, that gambling impacts more on lower income groups. In regions with lower incomes, we have, as a percentage, a higher share destined to gambling; it is money taken to businesses, to domestic consumption, taken also to care for children. We can't accept that. It is the task of the Republic to remove the obstacles of an economic and social order, which, in fact limiting the freedom and equality of the citizens, prevent the full development of the human person and the effective participation................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................... We should also pay attention to those 800,000 or perhaps 900,000 people (probably they are twice as many but we don't even have the data because serious epidemiological research is not funded), to those hundreds of thousands, if not millions of problematic players that a state collided with lobbying led to an unacceptable state of suffering, even more unacceptable when we think that to suffer more are not even the adults but the children. What about Article 32 of the Constitution, health protection? What's left of it? 11:32 a.m. (Follow ENDRIZZI). Today, in our young people and in their attitude towards gambling - according to a research by the Nomisma Institute, I repeat, presented in Bologna this summer - we already find the warning signs of compulsive behavior, from expectations to psychological values downloaded on gambling, to the chase of loss, which establishes the entry into a state that we could define as pathology, when the player, while realizing that he is impoverishing, sees no other solution than to continue to gamble, waiting for a big win (which we know will not be). It is paradoxical, in this situation, that conventions with multinationals oblige contractors to invest even in advertising, which then becomes a means of conditioning the freedom of the press. With 200 million per year of advertising investments, in fact, any economic entity can go to the editor of a newspaper or the chief editor and say: We want a less critical editorial line for us, otherwise we have to withdraw the investment. We also see it in the RAI-Radiotelevisione Italiana: the service contract has been suspended for more than a year and would foresee, at least in the public service, the abolition of advertising, but it is held firm. By who? For what, dear Mirabelli? It is held firm because there is no political will, while there are statements of façade; here we have had proof of the hypocrisy of the Government, because, if they really agreed on the public service, they would already have done so. That is why it is unacceptable to think of combining an agile, fast bill that responds to the needs of urgency and social and pedagogical emergency with another, which instead is not to know how many articles - say you, Mirabelli, how many are - and therefore will inevitably be subject to cross vetoes and negotiations, as we have seen recently on reforms and now on civil unions: What do you give me in return? "No, you vote for the reform, then we'll talk about it: if you want, I'll give you something on Italicum. We cannot accept this: it is not possible to trade the health of young people with any other consideration. We also know that abolishing advertising is also a means of affecting evasion and illegal gambling on sites, because a website without the possibility of being known is easily "sterilizable." We saw this summer the scandal of 1,500 offering points linked to the illegal sites operating on Malta, in connection with the 'ndrangheta'. In order to strike at this phenomenon, we must abolish advertising and here I believe that there will be a vote in favour of this House, which in its time has already voted on the moratorium on new games and an agenda in the RAI reform to abolish gambling advertising, which, as I have already said, is the tool to increase and induce new demand. Finally, President, today that we are talking about the bill Boccaduttri this I must say anyway, with this bill we will also go to make the image of politics cleaner, no longer having to submit to the shame of sponsorships, as we saw at the meeting of Communion and Liberation and at the Feast of Unity, where we had the stands that promoted gambling. Now I take it for granted that, after the House has voted unanimously, it will want to confirm this propensity. We need to start this path right away that has no costs, but only benefits. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u176	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, honourable representative of the Government, this measure, which was already approved in the Chamber of Deputies on 9 September 2015, consists of three articles and makes some changes to Article 9 of Law No 96 of 6 July 2012, which had halved the amount of electoral refunds to the parties and obliged them to revise and certify their financial statements by an external company registered in the register of listed companies and the CONSOB list. To this end, these companies, which can operate for a maximum of six consecutive years on the same party, must verify the regular keeping of the accounts and the correct recording of the management facts in the accounting records. They shall also have an obligation to check that the financial statements are in accordance with the records and records, the findings of the investigations carried out and the rules governing them. The 2012 law also established the Guarantee Commission on the statutes and for the transparency and control of the statements of political parties with the aim of ensuring their full operation, also in the light of the additional functions entrusted to them by Decree-Law No 149 of 2013 which amended the rules on electoral refunds in favour of political parties and movements in fact abolishing public funding to parties. The first task of this measure is to strengthen the staff of this Commission, which has so far proved insufficient to carry out the task for which it was established and thus to strengthen the power of control and supervision over parties and to increase their transparency in order to respond to a just and acceptable demand from public opinion and the national community. In particular, Article 1 (1) (a) allows the aforementioned Commission to be assisted in carrying out the tasks entrusted to it by five units dependent on the Court of Auditors, involved in the audit activity and by two units dependent on other public administrations experienced in the audit activity. This new staff complements the five members already envisaged by the Commission who are at the top of the three judges: the Council of State, the Court of Cassation and three members of the Court of Auditors who exercise their four-year term of office, renewable once without receiving any compensation. Such staff, which will strengthen the Commission's work in guaranteeing, transparency and control, shall be placed out of the role of their own administrations and shall benefit from the same annual gross economic treatment enjoyed at the time of their appointment, including ancillary allowances, paid to their respective administrations. In addition, in order to exclude the emergence of new or increased public finance charges, it shall be made unavailable for the duration of the out-of-the-job placement of the staff of which the Supervisory Board uses, a number of posts in the overall budget of the administration belonging to the equivalent financial point of view. Paragraph (b) of the same paragraph provides that the magistrates of the Commission for the duration of the relative assignment, are placed out of role by the administrations of belonging in accordance with the provisions of the so-called anti-corruption law on the matter. Paragraph 2 intervenes on a second aspect of this provision, previewing that the provision according to which, in the exercise of control over the statements of the parties, the Commission also verify the conformity of the expenses actually incurred and the revenues received to the documentation produced to proof of the same, well apply to the financial statements for the years after 2014, excluding therefore the financial years 2013 and 2014. Consequently, the Commission's report on the judgment of regularity and compliance with the law of the financial statements for the financial years 2013 and 2014 shall be carried out in accordance with that forecast. Paragraph 3 of Article 1 provides that, for the financial year 2013, the report, which should have been approved by 30 June 2015, should be rendered, on the basis of the new regulatory forecasts, within 30 days of the entry into force of the measure under consideration. It should be noted that the reason for this rule lies in the fact that, despite the fact that the parties have produced in the years 2013 and 2014 all the necessary documentation, according to article 9, paragraph 1, of the law of 2012, and therefore they have had their balance sheets examined by the same companies that also operate on the stock exchange, the guarantee commission has not been able up to now to exercise such substantial verification for lack of personnel and structures. I would therefore like to make it clear, in order not to provide bad information and thus disinformation, that the parties, all parties, have fulfilled exactly the legal obligations on the financing of politics. They presented their financial statements and documents to support the financial statements, which could justify each individual expenditure in the eyes of the external audit firm. Unfortunately, the Institutional Commission has failed to do its job, as denounced by the President of the Commission in a letter to the President of the Chamber of 30 June 2015, after the first Commission, set up in 2012, resigned in 2014, claiming that it is not in a position to carry out its work seriously. For these reasons, the parties were in a position to have fulfilled all the legal obligations and, blamelessly, to suffer the fact of the delays on the residual financing, money that serves to pay external creditors and employees for an activity actually carried out, budgeted, certified and controlled. The reimbursements will only be paid after budgetary control, including for 2013. They must do so within 30 days, of course, but it is assumed that an investigation work has already been done, since those budgets have been in the Commission's possession since June 2014. For the 2014 budgets, delivered a few months ago (in June 2015), the Commission will have until June 2016. The fourth paragraph, with a provision of authentic interpretation, deals with a delicate aspect, which concerns the protection of the rights of employees by parties. The parties, in fact - I remind those who listen to us - are an instrument guaranteed by a constitutional rule, Article 49, according to which it is established that all citizens have the right to join freely in parties in order to contribute democratically to determining national politics. For this reason, they deserve wide protection, therefore public, constitutionally guaranteed and not only private. We must not, in fact, think only of the great parties, but of any political subject, whether small or large. We must remember, as legislators, that a party is not only made up of ideological-value options or communication of what it thinks, but of personnel, structures, commitments and suppliers: it is made, in short, of workers. CASTALDI (M5S). And owned! Mr Gotor, rapporteur. It is a structure that engages subjects who perform an activity that must be paid and protected by the laws and social shock absorbers that regulate the life of the vast majority of Italian workers. For this reason, paragraph 4 specifies that the application to the staff of the political parties of the legislation on extraordinary treatment, wage integration, extraordinary earnings fund and solidarity contracts also operates with respect to the articulations and territorial sections of the parties, regardless of the number of employees, including those with legal and financial autonomy. The same paragraph 4 also amends article 4, paragraph 7, of the same decree-law of 2013, abolishes with retroactive effect the obligation for political parties to be registered in the relevant national register for the purpose of the use of the recalled social shock absorbers. Finally, Article 2 lays down the financial invariance clause and Article 3 lays down the time limit for the measure to enter into force. In conclusion, I would like to stress that I have specifically asked to be rapporteur for this measure because I am among the two or three Senators of the Democratic Party who in 2013 did not vote the law that abolished during this legislature, moreover by decree-law, public funding to parties. I am, in fact, radically opposed to the spirit of such a measure, which I consider to be the most serious and contradictory measure adopted in the current legislature for the costs which it will entail in the medium term on the proper and sound functioning of Italian democracy. In my opinion, the principle of public funding of parties is a principle of democratic guarantee which, not by chance, applies in all European democracies because of its characteristics of universality, neutrality, thirdness and proportionality, thus responding to an elementary need for justice. The parties, according to the Constitution, have a public function and a civil country cannot be limited to private funding because, if it does, it implicitly recognizes that politics must respond only and exclusively to private and particular interests. We should therefore have the dignity of defending the parties and not hypocritically smoothing the hair of public opinion, fueling a general and indiscriminate condemnation which almost equates them with criminal associations. On the contrary, the dignity of defending them means precisely engaging in spasimo to distinguish the wheat from the loglio, the bad plant of evil and delinquent deviation, moreover extremely representative - I say it with great bitterness - of Italian society, with a healthy trunk, that there is and that it must grow. This must start from the principle that, if everyone is a thief and if everyone steals in the same way, he opens the way to a general self-absolution that is especially appropriate to criminals and gives space to occult and informal centers, cliques of power able to better and more effectively condition an increasingly weak, irrelevant and dequalified policy in the eyes of public opinion. Unfortunately, I know parties and movements without or with poor internal democracy, but I do not know democracy in the West without strong and vital parties. The history of the twentieth century teaches us that there is no political subject with totalitarian vocation that has not taken power claiming not to be a party, but a movement of liberation from parties. This happened because, if in politics you refuse the concept of being a part, of taking part, then you have the claim to represent the whole and you end up wanting to be in the intimate conviction, totally unproved of course, of having a superiority, even moral, over others. The controversy against the parties and Parliament, which always presents itself as new and emerging, is actually as old in Italy as the cocco. To understand the origins of this national identity can help us a step of notebooks from the prison of Antonio Gramsci, who explains that the popular and mass force of this apoliticalism - he wrote - derives from the correlation with the national ruling classes, with the ethical-civil quality of our bourgeoisie, which has never had a national vocation, but rather either cosmopolitan or dedicated to detail. He writes Gramsci and quotes: A variety of this popular "apoliticalism" is the "almost" of the physiognomy of traditional parties, the roughly of programs and ideologies. So even in Italy there is a particular "sectarianism." (...) The sectarianism in the popular elements corresponds to the spirit of consort in the ruling classes, is not based on principles, but on passions even low and ignoble and ends with the approach to the "honorable point" of the underworld and the omerta of the mafia and of the camorra. This apoliticalism, combined with the representative forms (especially of the local elective bodies), explains the deteriority of political parties, in Italy, which were all born on the electoral ground. These are words written in a fascist prison in the thirties of the twentieth century, yet it seems that we are talking about the parties of about today, reduced to electoral cartels and a cramped practice of the democracy of the character: a federation of lonely men in command, with annexed procession, that deludes and promises, but is not able to solve the Italian problems, first of all that of selecting a ruling class to meet the challenges we face. It seems incredible to have to admit it, but today in Italy politics is the only - I repeat, the only - area of human action in which experience and professionalism not only do not matter, but are judged a disvalue. [[Applause from Senators Broglia, Buemi and Panizza]] They do not serve to obtain consent, rather they subtract it. Is it politics? Is it because of the poor quality of Italian politics? Of course, but this answer is not enough for me: it is absolutely partial, omissive and ultimately cowardly (I can now say it also from direct experience, having frequented different worlds). In short, politics cannot become a closed enclosure where one enters with the vote of fewer and fewer citizens, entrusted to oligarchies of wealthy and money that can afford it, or be fed by famous testimonials that allow to avoid the need for public funding. These are the reasons why I am the rapporteur for this uncomfortable measure, but let us do it seriously and without hypocrisy. I also say this to my party, because above all of this Italy needs: not to deceive its audience by riding the demagogy of the screaming from one illusion to another, but to proceed to a profound reform of the parties, serious and transparent, with democratic rules of operation within them, finally applying Article 49 of the Constitution With this measure, in the case of those workers that we feel for an elementary duty of solidarity to support, we remind those who listen that we are using a form of public funding, because the private one would not have been enough and would not have been used to respond to that unbearable duty of protection of those workers. At first sight it does not appear, but paragraph 4 of Article 1 of this provision has to do with the dignity of making politics and with the theme of the resources of politics, of the dignity of the workers and, therefore, with the enormous theme of the quality of our democracy and our civilization. It does not seem - perhaps it does not appear - but even so we try to defend an invisible line that slowly, how progressively, separates what remains of democracy from the new barbarians, those who are coming and will make the laws, those that the senators for inertia have not done, as the Greek poet Constantine Kavafis wrote. It is in this spirit, with these convictions and also with these concerns, that I submit this small but important measure to the evaluation of the Senate. [[Applause from the PD Group and Senators Buemi and D'Ascola. Congratulations. Comments from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u186	I envy those who, at this juncture, do not realize the complexity of which I was talking, the themes that go from Afghanistan to Nigeria, because it is a very diversified block, because it is an element of union: a red thread binds different pieces of our world, but this red thread is held together by the presence of an extremist movement that has obvious repercussions of a terrorist nature, that takes on diversified and different names, from Al Qaeda to ISIS and ISIL, to Boko Haram, but that is united by the desire to question the sense of the deeper values that animate us and animate the vast majority of the international community. Immigration is only a consequence of this and if we continue to deal with it simply with instinctive reactions, on the single day, without a wider scenario, we will never solve the great issue of our time. We have no right to talk about such delicate issues without feeling the heart crying, in front of a country like Jordan, which has four million inhabitants, of which 1,4 are refugees and refugees: the second city of Jordan is a refugee camp. How can we address this issue without facing the problem of Lebanon, where also our fellow countrymen, of whom we are proud, lead the UNIFIL mission with the commitment of the entire international coalition, but with the typical passion of the world of Italian professionalism? [[Applause from the PD, FI-PdL XVII, AP (NCD-UDC) and Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT) and PSI-MAIE Groups.]] How can we deal with this issue, even in relation to our own small internal raves, without feeling on us the pain of the wound of the world that is being immortalized and made evident by what has happened, for example, in Turkey these days? I have experienced the youth movement of a party and I think it is one of the most extraordinary realities when a young person approaches the commitment of the public. The images of those guys and those girls who took selfies a few moments before going to die in an attack like the Turkish one of a few days ago should ask us first of all the deep question about what makes sense in the public thing today. To some of us they probably remind Utøya and what happened in 2011 in Norway and should ask us the question about the deep sense of service to the public thing; but at the same time they should also ask us what kind of relationship we want to have with Turkey and against terror. Let's get back to that. The question of immigration Italy has set it up; then, it may or may not like it, you can accept or not accept the position of the Italian Government and the way in which the Government and the Ministries have managed this dynamic, but the question of immigration is not a question of series B: it is the answer to epochal phenomena that are going through our world and that have seen this Government start from a symbolic gesture. [[Comments of Senator Airola]] The first visit was not made by this government in Paris, Berlin, London, New York or Moscow: it was made in Tunis. Today I am pleased to share your and our joy in taking note that the 2015 Nobel Peace Prize went to Tunisian civil society. [[Applause from the PD, AP (NCD-UDC), Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE, AL-A and Mixed and Senator Bencini]] Able to fight for democracy, unique (at this time) Arab spring phenomenon that has had a positive echo. The European Council discusses this. It may seem strange, but the European Council is at a time of reflection on the profound sense of the identity of our continent, fifty-eight years after what happened in Rome, with the signing of the Treaty of Rome. The deep sense of European identity is called into question. Some of the countries that in the last fifteen years have become part of the great European family have imagined, in recent months, to play their identity in a key of opposition; some in a key of opposition with Russia - I will return shortly - others in a key of opposition that has symbolically and plastically highlighted itself through the creation of walls. My generation, but I believe the different generations of all of us, knows perfectly well that, if there is one element that has contributed more than others to creating a sense of European identity, this has been the path taken since the collapse of the Berlin Wall. All of us remember the deep emotion of that time, the impression that history was doing overtime and that it was going through exactly that time. Today, twenty-six years later, an incredible fact is happening: the European identity, built around the collapse of the Berlin Wall, is being questioned by countries liberated from that event, who imagine to erect a new wall, opposing fears to fears and imagining to build in negative the sense of their identity. It is on these issues that Italy must make its voice heard; it is on these issues that a solid, strong and responsible country is needed to be heard not in the traditional dimension of "let us go to Europe to defend our interests," but in saying that we are within Europe, which is our home, helping to have a European policy that is not the result of daily hysteria, but is linked to a multi-year horizon. That is why I think the European Council is important. I could certainly now discuss the substance of the fact that, for the first time, we accept the principle of hotspots, relocations on a European basis and returns as a European policy. It is an important, significant issue. For the first time 19 Eritrean boys, a few days ago, were accompanied to take a plane and brought from Lampedusa to Sweden. It is the beginning of a significant number of relocations, which represent a part of the European strategy but which are not important if they are not included in an overall reasoning. The overall reasoning is that at the moment Europe needs to have an attitude that is different from that of the past. It is not with the raids - imagined more to respond to their public opinions than to solve the problems - that they deal with the major issues of international politics. There is no doubt that, in the face of the terrorist threat, there is also the need to respond through the commitment of the military and a great international coalition, of which Italy is part, moreover, with rules of engagement shared in Parliament, indeed, bound by parliamentary decisions. The central point is that in some circumstances - all of you know what we are talking about - there have been moments when some interventions, designed more to settle their own internal public opinions, have produced results that are before everyone's eyes today. If we have to be more clear and say that the intervention in Libya in 2011 did not solve the problems of that country, not only do we say so here, but we will also say it tomorrow, in the presence of the Secretary-General of the United Nations, that the President of the Senate together with the President of the Chamber have appropriately invited, and in the presence - because he will be there too - of the UN Special Envoy to Libya, Bernardino Leon. We need more than just a list of actions: we need a strategy. The European Council will discuss this, particularly with attention on the great Syrian and Turkish scenarios, but at the same time noting that there is a need for a horizon that in the next ten to fifteen years brings Italy to be the protagonist, not everywhere (it cannot be), but in some parts of the world. The strategic line of this government provides large investments in Africa, because we think that over the next ten to twenty years that area of the world will be the most potentially interesting from the point of view of both growth and risks. In fact, we cannot think that phenomena of religious fanaticism or extremism and terror are confined only to Asian countries, because we see it. In fact, when we went to Kenya we all chose together that we could talk in a university after what happened in Garissa and, when we went to Tunis, we all chose to go and pay homage in a museum, a symbol of that city and that country. In fact, when we face a question as a country-system, we do not simply move in the logic of interest, which also exists and is strong, starting from the energy one and in the oil & gas sector, as it is obvious that having our main Italian company a particular interest in that sector, but we do so with a broader geopolitical gaze, which leads to say today that the stabilisation of Egypt, absolutely crucial in that quadrant, also passes from some interventions and investments that can make the Italian enterprise and reality. This is the first chapter of tomorrow's debate at the European Council: a major issue of foreign policy which has internal repercussions and which must be addressed today knowing that when Italy said six months ago that we could not do it alone, that we needed an overall strategy with a response for the immediate and one for the long term, this type of action and approach today is appreciated by the vast majority of the members of the European Union. They do not all accept it, because you know that there is also a very high discussion on this issue, and of course I respect those who are inspired by some countries that are our partners and allies. There are, in fact, those who call themselves a model to Orban or those who claim that their model is similar. I respect this position, but I believe that centuries of civilizations lead Italy to have a different position, that is, that of those who are proud of what they are doing in the Mediterranean with the Coast Guard and the Navy and that at the same time leads to say that the values of solidarity and acceptance that we have shown must be entered in a framework of rules that must finally be European. If, after six months, we can talk about a mission carried out in principle, let us not fool ourselves, because there is still a lot to do, because the scenarios that open before us will not be the simplest (after the European Council, on 11 and 12 November in Valletta there will be a big initiative with African countries; on 15 and 16 November the G20 will take place, this year in Turkey, then the new appointment of the European Council), but 2016 is open to a strong and solid response from Europe. To achieve this, the second concluding point of my introduction to the work of tomorrow addresses issues related to economic governance. The five presidents' report addresses issues related to the UK's stay in the European Union, the so-called referendum on Brexit, and also addresses issues related to strategic development policies in the coming years. Our government and I see it this way, and we say it without too many diplomatic tours: this debate can only make sense if we start from the assumption that in the last seven years (we refer to the beginning of the great crisis in the United States and then, consequently, in Europe) every evaluation that can be made must start from a fact that the economic policy of the European Union has not given the answers that were necessary. [[Applause from the PD, AP (NCD-UDC) and CoR groups. Comments of the Divine Senator.]] And this fact we say with bitterness, not with satisfaction, of those who know that, if we look together at American data and European data, in the policy of restarting within their borders, the United States has made far better performances. Perhaps it is time to say that a policy of just limiting expenditure, of just rigour and budgetary discipline, of austerity alone, is not enough to revive the economy, but above all the dynamism of a part of Europe that was to be, to follow, granting the evaluation of the Lisbon Strategy, the most brilliant and dynamic piece in the world. It is not; trivially, it was not. It is good then to discuss the governance and the report of the four Presidents (which has become of the five Presidents because the European Parliament has also wanted to get into this dynamic: and well to come), but the point is that every evaluation of governance must start from a consideration on what the strategy is. Before we discuss and discuss which pilot is the best, let's agree on which direction it is. Italy has a clear position and, paradoxically, for Italy an extraordinarily interesting period opens up, and much of the merit is yours, the Senate of the Republic. I come very briefly to explain why. Everything will happen in Europe over the next two years. [[Comments of the Divine Senator.]] In 2017 there will be a referendum in the United Kingdom, elections will be held in France (which are expected to be particularly important) and there will be elections in Germany, after a European scenario where you have seen what has happened in Portugal and Greece (and we will see what will happen in Spain and Poland within a few months). The point is that 2017 is a bit the key date, the watershed. The five Presidents' report also divides between a first part of the short term, potentially feasible immediately, and a second part of the potential revision of the Treaties that is imagined for the follow-up. In this biennium, Italy is more solid and, paradoxically for the recent history of our country, a point of reference in terms of stability. Italy has stopped being considered the problem. Then we can discuss, in the external tale, whether the problem was for its own responsibility or for a different reading that came from our partners and friends; but this is not the place to open a discussion. The fact is that we were perceived as the problem. We were asked to do our homework. This country was charged with a series of alleged shortcomings (presumed or real, then each will evaluate) which led, paradoxically, to the European political debate being deprived of the Italian voice. Every time we posed our problems, our strategy or our solutions, the prejudice was as follows: Before, though, worry about you. You say it for yourselves. Ask for the Growth Pact, rather than the Stability Pact, because it is at your heart. Well, this country shows up in Europe with the debt curve that, since 2016, returns to fall, after nine years. This country presents itself in Europe having done a reform of the labour market within a year, with opinions, as is well known, different in this House, while the fastest and fastest countries (and I am referring to Germany) took three years. This country has made institutional reforms (electoral law, public administration reform) and proudly has constitutional reform, with a more or less shared principle, as is well known, but which gives great solidity to the institutional system. Ours is the Government number 63 and I am the President of the Council number 27 of the history of the Republic in almost seventy years. [[Senator Centinario's comments.]] If the reforms go to completion, as is conceivable and, on our side, desirable, we will have a mechanism in which the President-in-Office of the Council changes once every five years and not once a year. In view of this attitude, which is clear to international public opinion and, above all, to our governments, what does reality say? He says that if Italy continues to do what it is doing, we are paradoxically the only ones or almost on the side of stability. Let us face this two-year period with an attitude in which we can bring our voice, without worrying to hear the others say "you have to do this or that." The growth of Italy is back to being positive and the prospects of our country are back to being inspired by the good weather and not by the storms, and then, in the light of this, there is a great responsibility that we must bring to Europe, that is not simply to do things at home, but to help this continent to understand, on immigration, foreign policy and economic policy, if we simply want to be a set of 507 million individuals united only by a common geographical belonging or if we finally want to return to give Europe a soul. That's why the year 2017 will be very interesting. We will have a greater responsibility: we will not have elections, we will have the G7, we will have in that year the sixty years of the Treaty of Rome, on which the Foreign Minister has already begun a discussion with the five founding countries, to start from the Treaty of Rome and try to have a wide-ranging debate, and all this recalls the individual responsibilities. I have finished, Mr President, tomorrow's European Council is not decisive, it is good to say so. [[Comments of Senator Castaldi),]] But it is a European Council in which anyone who wants to hear the voice of countries that are finally back to being proud of what they are doing. I represent a country, not a government in that case, but a country that hasn't changed its mind. Not many people on immigration can say they haven't changed their minds. Not a lot of people who don't have to justify the turnarounds of the last six months. I represent a country that has a solidity of values that should first of all make us excited by the beauty it expresses, a country that in the next two years will be able to play a key role, also thanks to the fact that women and men who sit in this House, regardless of their political color, have courageously chosen, even in spite of the forecasts of the first year of legislature, to make this a legislature really capable of writing the word end to structural reforms. If we are able in the next two years to finally bring politics to the Brussels palaces, politics not simply as a national interest, but as an interest of the great European homeland to regain a role on issues that have been left to the hysteria of the day after day and to the daily apprehension of public opinion and the media, without a role of foreign policy, we will be consistent with our history of Italians and of Italians and above all we will be able to help Europe. I believe that if there is something new after this long period, it is that Europe has stopped considering Italy as the problem: today Italy can be a part of the solution of European problems. It'll depend on the work of all of us. [[Applause from the PD, AP (NCD-UDC), Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT) and PSI-MAIE and AL-A groups.]]
