ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u195	BONFRISCO (CoR). Mr President, we are well aware that tomorrow's European Council will be a new test for Europe and you go with a smile - what makes you so nice to the Italians - of those who may have said so and of those who, as you proudly mentioned earlier, do not need to change their position: others have had to do so, in defining a global strategy on the subject of immigration, with an effort that goes beyond emergency interventions, responding to the challenge of massive migratory flows, so as to support overall and determined action over time. This you asked, and this is probably - I hope - going to get our country tomorrow. I wonder if the debate at the European Council, which will be held tomorrow, would have had the content that it probably will have had if there had not been the Syrian crisis, if there had not been tens and tens of thousands of refugees crossing Eastern Europe for the first time and not just the shores of our sea. I wonder, in summary, if this did not happen because of absurdity, what the behaviour of Europe would have been today: alas, probably, what it has been for many years, of substantial indifference to what was happening in the Mediterranean, of isolation for what concerned us, of abandonment and absolute disregard for problems that are certainly of epochal nature - you are right to remember - as such; but it is even more serious that Europe, even in the face of epochal problems, never has the courage to take the chorus by the horns. President. - The next item is the report (Doc. We are told that the content of the Treaty of Dublin is finally being called into question and that this will be our merit, a merit of Italian firmness on this front. But then, excuse me, Mr President, why did you not allow the amendments tabled to the constitutional reform, which specifically asked for the possibility of revising, revising and reevaluating those treaties by making the choice of the Italian people, to be accepted? Mr President, the data from the European Frontex Agency are clear and are not exactly the picture that Mr Repetti has now painted: in the first nine months of this year 710,000 migrants have illegally crossed the borders of the European Union, while in the same period of the previous year there were 282,000 illegal crossings. Italians cannot be fooled by saying that for Italy the situation has gone better because the decrease in arrivals that occurred last month - I know that Minister Alfano is very fond of this data - is mainly due to the bad weather and the lack of boats in relation to the demand that remains very strong of crossing the channel of Sicily. We now expect serious commitments for a revision of the Dublin Regulation and for better control of the external borders with the emergence of a Community border security force, but we are already alarmed by the words of Undersecretary Gozi, who is talking about a commitment to gradual integrated border management. Why is the adjective "graduale" used, Undersecretary Gozi? There is nothing gradual; there is an emergency that is even epochal. It is a real invasion and, as such, must be treated and addressed. Mr President, next spring, in fact, we expect a new and more massive wave if the situation in the Middle East does not change for the better - which is not easy to predict - and unfortunately all the data we receive indicate, instead, that it will get worse. The data that the Ministry of the Interior makes public is still at March 30 of this year and, President, I ask you why these data are still at that date, but the projection that you can easily deduce comes to the figure of about 150,000 for 2015. These are the numbers we need to deal with when we talk about immigration. Beyond his considerations, there are also considerations on the vulnerability of our borders and also on how to strengthen our security because the greatest attention must be given, in our opinion, to cooperation between services and intelligence that must help to defend the borders of Europe and our country as a whole. Mr President, do you know what happens in Gorizia? Do you know what happens at the train stops that go to the Brenner, from Verona to the North? If you have it told by the members of that railway police who assist every day in passing through the trains, where a minimum part is stopped, moreover according to criteria of logistic convenience, and a great one is dispersed. They disperse into a territory that already has heavy repercussions. In our opinion, without adequate measures, it is clear that in such a scenario, the Middle East crisis that we all know can be plunged even further into the European Union, even in an irreparable way. That is why we are calling for a more rigorous and timely policy on immigration and security, but not gradual. Italy cannot afford wrong or superficial and less ideological assessments, because under the aegis of the UN and within the precise framework of the United Nations and NATO every effort must be made to ensure the security of our country and of Europe as a whole. There is already NATO, Mr President, and we must strengthen our commitment within that context. We cannot get lost by doubling bureaucratic structures and costs in other joints: we have already made this mistake in Libya and Syria and we risk doing so in Egypt if there were not, thank God, a strong Italian presence of an economic type that perhaps will protect us from this risk. In common with them we have a sensitivity and a human trait that are perhaps the best response. Without politics there is no weapon that holds, as only politics can win. We have a cooperation that we have come back to develop - I thank Minister Gentiloni for having given her great impulse - and that must become the figure of Italians in that area to allow us to return to be the protagonists of that chessboard. If we are to ensure peace and security, we need growth and development policies for us and for those who are close to us. Hence the need to defend borders through the possible creation of European border guards. The problem is that in Italy these pull factor are many and constantly increasing. ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u204	Mr Barani (AL-A). Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the speech by the President-in-Office of the Council was brief but intense. The points raised are those of the European Agenda (immigration, the situation in the Mediterranean, the document of the five Presidents), but the tone has not always been uniform. It is good to remember the delays with which Europe has become aware of the problem of that great exodus represented by immigration; it is also good to claim the battles sustained by Italy when others did not see or did not want to see, and then suddenly to be faced with a rising tide, which - like the tide in fact - in the end spares no one. Similarly, when the emergency became continental, a fact that was not only about the southern border of Europe, the existence of a problem and - if I may say so - a common destiny has been rediscovered. The sudden decisions of Angela Merkel in the sign of acceptance have modified a perception previously felt only by some, but then that great gesture of solidarity had to deal with the data of a reality difficult to master, until determining reactions of opposite sign. And that is when that request for solidarity, which had been unheard of by Italy alone or by the majority of Greece, turned into a common cry. In the meantime, however, valuable and necessary time had been wasted to prepare the necessary measures to deal with an emergency that has the characteristics we know and that cannot be said to be resolved by recent decisions that have led to the distribution of refugees among all European countries. In this case, in fact, the disproportion of numbers is evident: we can ensure a better fate for those 160,000 unfortunate, but what about the other million? How can we guarantee them a minimum of assistance or provide for their most basic needs? This is still the great unknown that weighs on the European debate, provided that this wants to be the child of a frank and not comfortable debate, built only to dump on some a problem that is rather general. The hope is therefore that the European summit tomorrow and after tomorrow, Mr President, will not end with the use of weapons of minimalism or postponement, but will take up the real facts, and this will be the result of an operation of truth. In his speech, the President-in-Office reminded us of the forthcoming electoral deadlines for several European countries: Spain, France and Germany. Many will interpret this hint as a putting their hands forward. It will be said that this is not the time for strong decisions, that we must wait for the situation to decay. For our part, we do not believe that this is the case, that this must be the case. We can't wait for 2017 to take action. Because otherwise, by then, the situation risks being out of control. In order to prevent this from happening, it is necessary to identify a reception strategy based on actual facts. On an intelligence that considers, in a realistic way, the effective flow of immigration, which distinguishes refugees from simple migrants for economic reasons and behaves accordingly: providing forms of assistance for the former, on the basis of international rules, and the immediate return for the latter. At the same time, the necessary financial resources will have to be provided, which cannot be burdened on the individual state, but must be found at European level. These are the immediate measures to be taken to deal with what we might call a planning of despair. However, quota measures represent only a horn of the dilemma. It is clear that the phenomenon is destined to continue, at least until the black caliphate is militarily defeated. I greatly appreciated the vision of the President-in-Office of the Council. The connection, the link as he would have preferred to say, between the problems that connect the Mediterranean to Africa, and this area of the world to continents even further away, such as South-East Asia or territories of Russia, inhabited mainly by Muslims: Putin's great concern that drives him, beyond the traditional strategy of Santa Mother Russia, to intervene so hard in the main theatre of the conflict. With this new reality - that of ISIS - we have to deal with it. Trying to understand its essence, which is the indispensable prerequisite for developing a correct strategy. The President of the Council speaks of fanaticism and religious extremism. There is, of course, this too. But I am not convinced that this is the main key to interpretation. Even at the cost of sinning Eurocentrism I agree with Giuliano Amato, when he reminds us that this movement resembles a drop of water to the Nazism that has bloodied Europe. In the 1930s, books were burned as an expression of decadent art. But the hierarchs were making art. ISIS destroys the oldest vestiges in human history. Testimonies of epochs preceding the same monotheistic religions. The Nazis tortured and resorted to the horrors of genocide. ISIS does the same by using the media world as a scenario of a delusion of omnipotence. A form of attraction for thousands of disbanded - foreign fighters - who from faraway countries increase the ranks of terror. These similarities would suffice. But Nazism was above all a vocation to territorial expansion. The world seen as a great territory of conquest on which to wave the hooked flags. It was the great fault of the powers of then - France and England above all - that with the Covenant of Monaco they could not stop in time that monster that was advancing. We can't make the same mistake. Those armies - the ISIS armies - must be stopped and defeated. Good luck if you can. But I doubt it. And then with the use of force, to avert an even greater tragedy. That is the role of Italy and Europe. Contribute to the birth of this awareness among the great friendly powers. To ensure that the United States and Russia can find, together with NATO, a moment of convergence against the main enemy. We know that interests are not convergent. But neither were they during World War II. Yet Roosevelt chose Stalin to fight Hitler, except to deal with the Soviet Bear when the main enemy was eliminated. We have to repeat the same pattern. Is Assad, the Syrian president, an obstacle? Of course. But the accounts with him can be made later, when the weapons will remain silent and the black caliphate will be forced to surrender. What is the alternative? The final disappearance of Syria as a sovereign state? The division of that territory between Turkey, Iran and Saudi Arabia? Even if it were, the black caliphate, not militarily defeated, will continue to exist on the conquered territories. It will continue to be a center of global subversion. A nine-headed hydra capable of poisoning with her breath, as Greek mythology tells us, every part of the planet. It will be at the time the celebration of Assad's power, supported by Putin. Russia alone can hardly win this war. But alone she is able to militarily defend a space that she considers vital. As you can see, Mr President-in-Office, the problems are extremely complex. They require tenacity, patience and a strong ability to convince. It is a hard test for Italy and for Europe, but a challenge to which we cannot give up if, as in the case of immigration, we do not want to be overwhelmed, in the short space of a few months, by far more destabilizing phenomena. The hope is therefore that she will be able to do her part, bearing witness to a position that is shared - at least as I hope - by the majority of the Senate. For the rest, I can only share your reservations. The five Presidents' document is an important fact, but it is only if it is included in a broader reflection on the fate of Europe in the major areas of the planet. Out of this perspective, it is only a bureaucratic act, inadequate to deal with the real problems of an increasingly anemic continent, where austerity policies have created a desert, denying the future to most of its peoples who today, although confused, rebel in the illusory quest for sudden breaks that would transform a ship misplaced, as is Europe today, into a boat in the ocean of globalization. To populism in ambush we must respond by taking up the path of development, using solidarity between the different States until yesterday sacrificed on the altar of selfishness without prospects. The conditions for a change of pace are all there, starting with Germany, where the Volkswagen case and the Deutsche Bank case have demonstrated all the limitations of an economic policy that is geared towards accumulating surplus balance of payments, which has depressed the rest of the European economy. We hope that the lesson has served and that we can move as soon as possible to a revival of the domestic demand of that country, as locomotive that can drive the economy of the other countries, thus contributing to a stabilization of the whole of Europe. Mr President, we will be with you tomorrow, because we know that with the reforms you are moving forward you can make the interest of Europe and, above all, of Italian citizens. The reform of the Senate is also an important message given to all our international partners, not only to those of a political nature. There is satisfaction on our part with a large majority vote, therefore with great attention to our specificity also from the Government, but there is also an important expectation for how these reforms will make the Italian voice stronger, especially in the European context, where we want - as you said today - less rigour, less austerity and more growth, starting with the Stability Pact. Every year we ask for its relaxation in order to be able to use the huge resources unduly blocked: we are waiting for the law of stability to understand if there is from the Government the will to unlock at least a part of these resources that serve as the air that you breathe. Now that one, albeit minimal, partial derogation to meet the development needs of the state has managed to tear it away from Europe, we ask it to drop part of the derogation obtained on the territorial authorities, who suffer from the same situation and need to carry out interventions on indispensable structures. ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u219	FACTORS (M5S). Thank you, Mr President, and thank the Government. Four months ago we were here discussing the European Council in which we were going to talk about immigration emergency and today we are again talking about another European Council in which we are talking, once again, about immigration emergency, so all this self-celebration of how we are good at solving problems maybe I would leave it aside and I would say rather that the emergency has become structural, so let us try to stand on the ground. The government tells us that it was just asking for a revision of the Dublin regulation. We must say that the 5 Star Movement has been alone, in these years, asking for the same things. In 2014 we saw the adoption of our OSCE resolution in which we specifically asked for the revision of the Dublin Regulation, in December the motion Di Stefano, in which we asked the government to commit itself to the overcoming of Dublin: appeals essentially unheard. Those who were alone, therefore, in spite of the demagogy of this Parliament who blames us who knows what involvement in the problem of immigration, were us. We have always dealt with the problem very intelligently, making proposals that are mostly unheard of. We were alone even when we went around to control the various right and left cooperatives that managed the phenomenon of immigration that, as we remember, yielded more money than drugs. We were alone when we presented some exhibits for the incorrect custody of these contracts, I myself submitted a statement years ago for a reliance on the cooperative La Cascina, a question to which the Minister replied that the contract was very well given, well a few months later the arrests arrived. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]] So let us say that our contribution was important both internally and externally, we were right both internally and internally, with regard to the ill-management of immigration by cooperatives of all colors, we were right at European level to ask for those few points that you are asking for today: the overcoming of the Dublin Regulation, the establishment of a European asylum system with mutual recognition of asylum procedures, humanitarian corridors, hotspots. These are all things we've been asking for for a long time. Let us not be offended if you make our proposals yours, indeed we feel strengthened by the fact that when we rule we will have good ideas and we will be far-sighted. However, let us turn to the substance, because I like to be on the piece and tell you what we are asking of you today and what we are asking of you to vote in favour. It is true that Europe is beginning to think that immigration is not only an Italian problem, but this - I am sorry, Mr President-in-Office of the Council - is not due to the strength of Italy, it is simply due to the fact that winter has begun and migrations no longer take place by sea, as well as to the fact that the Syrian civil war has intensified and therefore refugees from Syria prefer to follow the path of the Balkans, thus touching those countries of the East that are so dear to Frau Merkel and that have a greater weight than us in the European chessboard precisely for the German-centric European Constitution. So let's settle down. We want to be much more incisive at European level in order to bring the demands of the whole Mediterranean area; because only when the Eastern countries move, putting in difficulty the Merkel and all the balances of the countries of that area, does Europe make itself felt. We are looking for a new rebirth and a new collaboration between the Mediterranean countries. Among other things, the issue was dealt with in the 14th Commission and a resolution voted unanimously. This is our first point: the real overcoming of the Dublin regulation. In fact, despite what you have said, the Dublin Regulation is still in force, no one has passed it; and it is not surpassed by quotas, because in this case too, the procedure for the analysis of asylum applications is always the responsibility of the country of first reception. We, on the other hand, want a European system for examining asylum applications. As regards the decision of the Justice and Home Affairs Council, which last 22 September adopted by a large majority the decision to introduce temporary measures in the field of international protection, with the famous quotas, I note that it is a naturally acceptable measure, but totally inadequate. Mr President-in-Office of the Council, 19 Eritreans are nothing. This summer I was in the center of Baobab and in Rome Tiburtina to see how many Eritreans there are in those places; these measures are not enough, because those men are subjected to a human trafficking that we, we Italians (not us, of course, but you, more than anything else: we, for the moment, are innocent and with clean hands). We would like to have a comment on how he is dealing with this organized underworld, which still persists in our territory and manages immigrants by making more money than with drugs. We therefore call for external action, but we also want a strong position on internal action. How do you intend to stop this human trafficking on which you have all profited? The third point of the European Council is the famous more for more; it means that we are going to help the development of the countries from which migrants come who have no right to asylum and, by helping them, we are being helped in return. It's okay, but the problem is internal: if we take 18 months to examine these people's application for asylum (so that we can make it better), it's clear that the more for more cannot work. So if you do not reform the internal asylum application system, the more for more will be absolutely inefficient. How do you want to reform this system? We have proposals: we have asked to strengthen the territorial centres in which asylum applications are examined. There is a bill of the 5 Star Movement, deposited in 1st Commission (and I invite Senator Finocchiaro to immediately question it), written across the board: it comes from the school of legislation ISLE, passed through the blog and is, therefore, a wonderful example of participatory democracy, to which all your assistants participated. If you do not want to discuss it because it is of the 5 Star Movement, sign it yourself: we do not care, as long as this problem resolves. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] I therefore call on the Commission to immediately schedule a draft law reforming the right of asylum, strengthen the territorial commissions and significantly shorten the time that an asylant is waiting for to see his or her procedure accepted and possibly to be repatriated; if it is not, he or she will be granted all the guarantees of an asylant. We have also made a proposal, Mr President-in-Office of the Council: if you listen to me, I will make proposals because we want to be proactive. We have proposed that 15,000 young people should be educated to examine asylum applications and put into territorial centres: this would solve youth unemployment on the one hand, and, on the other, we would form people to the practice of reception, which must be strengthened, however, because the committees currently work at low speeds. The reference to investments in these countries makes me a little 'afraid, as I am afraid that Senator Casini names Descalzi: let's remember that ENI does many good things, but indirectly finances the Libyan factions through the Libyan National Bank. We Italian State, therefore, indirectly finance ISIS. We don't do hypocrisies, say we have to fight ISIS, and then we go and invest by giving the money to the Libyan factions. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] This too would require meditation and self-criticism. I say this with regard to the issue of immigration, on which the Government knows very well that, beyond the facecies of SEL senators, it can count on our active cooperation. Therefore, the three points that we propose to the Government to consider positively - and I think that at this point they will not be able to refuse because they are very acceptable - are to strengthen its action in European fora in order to have a common asylum system, to support the possibility that refugees can apply for asylum in their countries of origin (i.e. the famous humanitarian corridors) and finally the system of binding (and not optional) quotas while overcoming the Dublin Regulation. Let us remember that quotas do not constitute an overrun of the Dublin Regulation. You have partly glissed on the document of the five Presidents. We have also examined it. It is currently in the 14th Commission. It is the famous document of the four Presidents, which has now become of the five Presidents, because - goodness to them - they have included the President of the European Parliament, the only elected body, who was perhaps the only one to be present in this text. It is a document that leaves us a little perplexed, because in fact it asks the member countries to further surrender sovereignty in a multistep which first involves an economic and monetary union and then as the last step, a political union. The problem is that we are building Europe and losing sovereignty with a wrong priority: if we have to give up sovereignty, we have to do it mainly for a political union and then in the alternative, perhaps if we create a Europe like that of the dream of the founding fathers who, with the famous Crocodile Club, had dreamt of a Europe that is not this but is different, then perhaps we can proceed to a monetary union. As for reforms, they are beautiful when they are right. You have become overwhelmed by anxiety and a paradoxism of reforms. It's kind of like when that comedian says the answer's inside you, but it's wrong. The reforms you are making are not what Europe is asking of us. Europe is calling for serious labour reform, not fake permanent contracts when we know very well that they are not. We are asking for a reform of small and medium-sized enterprises, a revival of the economy. We are asking you for citizenship income, the only instrument that can really boost consumption, but you are bringing us reforms that go against them. If you want a more democratic Europe and it answers us with a more oligarchical formula, the only solution is to increase democratic spaces, but you are narrowing them down. So in words you say you want a Europe of peoples, a Europe of democracy, but in fact you are obeying the Europe of banks and the Europe that wants to erase democracy. What we are asking of you at the fourth point in the resolution is therefore to analyse in a coherent way the document of the five Presidents. The last point concerns the question of Cameron asking the British if they want to leave the European Union. In our motion for a resolution we call for the Treaties to be reviewed so that there is the possibility of opting out of the single currency, i.e. the eurozone, or the European Union, and for the citizens to be called upon to speak (this happened only in 1989). We need to know what the Italians think of this Europe and only they have the right to decide what Europe they want. Our vote will abstain on all the motions for resolutions, not because there are no acceptable points, but because there are some that we cannot accept, such as linking autocratic constitutional reform to a democratic Europe. We will of course vote in favour of our motion for a resolution and ask the Government to accept at least the three points on immigration, which are exactly the same as those on the majority motion for a resolution, also to give a sign of cooperation between all political forces. We also want a further sign with the timetable for the draft law in the first Commission. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

Mr President, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, ladies and gentlemen, I was particularly impressed by your speech: you say that political decisions must not be triggered by emotions. I agree. It's just that this philosophy that you set out clearly then, as often happens to you, doesn't put it into practice. She is a great affabulator and often touches the sensitive cords of the soul, evoking emotions, stirring dreams. So often to understand his thought it is necessary to give a dreamlike reading of what he says. But the border that divides dreams from nightmares is a thin red line and it takes very little to cross it. You imagine a world, but you realize that you live in another one. And the endless resources of rhetoric, of which you abound, unfortunately do not change reality. You said that with the extraordinary European Council in April 2015, in the wake of the emotion of the death of 700 people, the European Union realizes that the problem of immigration can only be tackled with a strategic vision, that the Italian Government had understood it and that, in fact, the Dublin Agreement is over and, in this context, it exalts precisely the vision of the Italian Government. I'm sorry, but I'm of the opposite opinion. In her tale, in her narration - to use an expression that she likes - individual the limits of our weakness: the immigration emergency is not born this spring, but explodes in dramatic terms with the Syrian and Libyan crises. The problem existed, indeed, but the end of the Gaddafi regime - just look at the numbers - has transformed the physiological immigration of the poor who hope for a better future in an exodus determined by fear and terror. Well, our country, which is on the verge of drama, has failed neither with politics nor with striking gestures to draw Europe's attention to the problem. And the fact that the European Union has moved little, because little is involved, just because it is affected by the emotions of so many dramas, demonstrates the ineffectiveness of Italian action. The fact that the European Union does not exist at international level adds to the Italian foreign policy which can only be described with one adjective: inertial. We're still. Moreover, when you say that it is wrong for those who wake up in the morning to bomb in Syria, but it is also wrong for those who exclude the total procurement of the Syrian issue to Russia and its allies, in fact, stands in the middle. That is, it indicates a singular path: we must stand still, inert, for fear of error. But those who take no risk have no right to be heard. So you can meet Hollande in Modena and have dinner at Unity Festival, but you are not invited to the European Trilateral on Syria (I wouldn't have talked about it if you didn't bring the matter up in the House today). There, there were only France, Germany and England and Mogherini. And don't be mad at Mogherini if European partners only use us for catering. It is the result of an Italian foreign policy that is not there. Yeah, to count you have to move, you have to be there. You have to risk it. You see, I don't claim Craxi's courage from you to Sigonella. Nor that of Berlusconi who brought Bush and Putin to Pratica di Mare. But a foreign policy does not end with a trip to Moscow. If anything - to use the analysis of another former President who is very close to you, Romano Prodi - you have to have the courage to tell a raw truth: in Syria to beat the caliphate for the moment you have to rely on Russia and the soldiers of Assad. Foreign policy, if you remember, although it cannot be liked, is often a matter of priority. It is not exhausted, precisely, neither with goodness, nor with emotion, but with realism. There is a need to create a coalition that includes Europe, the United States, Russia, China and moderate Arab countries, if we want to intervene in that area of the world, so destabilized as to cause everyone, even the Pope, to worry. Take Libya. I am happy with the commendable humanitarian commitment of our soldiers, but that is certainly not the solution to the problem. That's a palliative, an aspirin. The real problem is that we must give back a state, an order to that country. And on this issue too, his approach is unique. You say: let's not repeat the mistake made in Libya in 2011. Well, I'd go and see what happened then. I heard in this House a few months ago a somewhat imaginative and biased reconstruction. I just shut up so I wouldn't get into any controversy. But apart from that - I spell out the words - the problem is not to repeat the error, but to remedy it. If we fail to solve the problem with today's times, cancer will spread, metastases will multiply, as you yourself say, from Afghanistan to Nigeria, from Kenya to Libya. And cancer must be eradicated with caution but also with determination. And never as on this front Italy has to do its part. Otherwise, as we see in Libya, a German UN envoy will succeed a Spanish envoy. One slap after another. And we will be unarmed actors, protagonists of paradoxical statistics. I have read a surreal one about the European Union's fight against the scafists: 38 million euros have been spent and 16 scafists have been arrested. In other words, every scafist arrested cost us €2.4 million. I hope that's not true. Same speech on the European Union. I agree with you. Europe has made a mistake in its anti-crisis strategy. To get out of the crisis we adopted four years after the policies successfully chosen by the United States four years earlier: we chose rigour, without accompanying it with development and growth, and we fixed the error only thanks to the commitment and courage of Draghi to the European Central Bank. But we paid for this delay. The road that divides Rome from Brussels is full of failures of companies and suicides of Italian entrepreneurs. In order to get out of the crisis, the European Union has made the policy that only a Member State, perhaps the strongest, namely Germany, was able to make use of. We have, in fact, also paid for the crisis in Germany. If you have time, read the proceedings of the trial to the rating agencies that are taking place in Trani: maybe you won't repeat the mistakes that most of the political class in this country made then. But while this reading of the facts is becoming a historical reality, she still repeats to me an analysis that echoes phrases of the type "we have to pass the exams". His reasoning is still imbued with pro-European rhetoric: one who has nothing against Europe says so. The truth is that Europe is still governed by national selfishness. And, never before, in order not to make Italians hate Europe - as happened yesterday in France and tomorrow in England - the Italian Government in Brussels must do first and foremost the interests of its own country. He must have the courage to make his voice heard, to say of the "no"s. Nor can he say that we are doing the reform of our Constitution to make us listen in Europe. This is absurd, not to say worse. Please don't say it: you don't change a Constitutional Charter with a Parliament delegitized by the Council; with a constituent majority that to date, just read the polls, it represents just over 30% of Italians and with all the opposition that leaves the House, that is, in other words, dividing the country just to please Europe. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII and Senator Centinario]] To adopt such a logic on the rules is absurd. If, then, you tell me that we are changing the Constitution to be heard, to have 8 billion flexibility on the budget from the European Union, we are really in disrepute. We are faced with an aberrant alibi. The Constitution, the fundamental Charter of a people, is priceless. Let's leave the dreams and get to the important issues. If we are to give substance to this debate, we must pursue a number of objectives. First of all, we must take full note of the end of the Dublin Treaty and, consequently, of the need to think of new rules that do not penalise the countries of first arrival, such as Italy. Secondly, we must open refugee camps directly on Libyan soil to prevent the Mediterranean from continuing to be a sea of death. Third point. We must urge the UN Security Council to launch the third phase of EUNAVFOR Med, which allows us to set foot on Libyan soil to fight the merchants of death. We must then consider the possibility of supporting diplomatic efforts, including military efforts to stop the Daesh. Finally, we must ensure that the five Presidents' document does not become a further loss of national sovereignty. [[Applause from the FI-PdL XVII and LN-Aut groups. Congratulations.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u225	MARAN (PD). Mr President, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, ladies and gentlemen, the work of the European Council will be devoted to the presentation of the work carried out on the report by the five Presidents on the completion of Economic and Monetary Union with a view to a wider debate at the December European Council, the process under way in view of the British referendum and, above all, the issue of migration. In this respect, the Summit will examine the implementation of the guidelines set out at the meeting on 23 September, agree further guidelines on priority actions within the European Agenda for Migration and take stock of the outcome of the Eastern Mediterranean-Western Balkans Conference on 8 October and preparations for the Valletta Summit on 11 and 12 November. The summit will conclude with an appointment dedicated to Turkey and Syria and Germany has asked to add the issue of Ukraine to the agenda. Here, the mere reading of the summit agenda says very long about the season that we are living, about the knots that are now coming to the comb and about how intricate the skein to unravel is. As a matter of fact, it also says a lot about the cheerful unawareness of some of our colleagues. We must recognise that progress is not lacking: on the migration front, as you have rightly pointed out, the facts of recent months are encouraging convergence on our positions not only of the European Commission, but also of our main partners, beginning with Germany and France, both on the internal front - on overcoming the Dublin system, which must remain our short-term objective - and on the external one, which is longer-term. The external dimension of migration and refugees is becoming increasingly important in the perception of the Union and of individual Member States. The Communication on the role of the Union's external action, adopted by the Commission on 9 September, recalls that political and diplomatic efforts are needed to address the challenges posed by the migration and refugee crisis, starting with the resolution of crises in Syria and Iraq. In its communication of 23 September, the Commission referred significantly to a diplomatic offensive, based on four main elements: High-level conference on the Eastern Mediterranean-Western Balkans route, which took place on 8 October; The Valletta Conference, scheduled for 11 and 12 November, as I said; greater operational cooperation with third countries; greater support for humanitarian organisations and agencies. The Eastern Mediterranean and Western Balkans Route Conference was a constructive opportunity for coordination between the Union and all countries affected by the refugee crisis. We must now ensure that the agreed actions are actually carried out, particularly with regard to cooperation with Turkey, whose role is central. The success of the Valletta Conference must be a priority for us: we must move as soon as possible to the adoption of concrete development projects, including joint initiatives by several Member States, aimed primarily at mitigating the root causes of immigration. At the Valletta Summit, the agreement establishing an emergency trust fund for Africa could be signed, which would finance projects for economic development, food security, management of migratory flows and governance in the three regions of Sahel, Horn of Africa and North Africa. At the Malta Summit, the Union will also have to raise without hesitation the issue of return, which is a key component of a real internal partnership and Italy must work to establish a positive conditionality between these issues and those of promoting regular migration and mobility. The long march of Syrian and Iraqi refugees towards Europe and the trafficking of human beings from the coasts of Tripoli on Lampedusa and Sicily also reminds us - as you have also raised - that Italy's national security, including energy, cannot be guaranteed without a geopolitical vision that has its natural fulcrum in the Mediterranean. Moreover, that the national interest of our country is the southern shore has been clearly written in the White Paper for defence and has also been confirmed by recent gas discoveries off Egypt, which impose on Italy a role of strategic partner for Cairo, both from the political point of view and of common security. The Euro-Mediterranean region, we wrote, represents the main area of national intervention and must achieve a high degree of stability and democratic development in countries that affect the Mediterranean, because it is a priority objective for our country. What is happening, it must be acknowledged, and the agenda of the Council's work confirms the validity and correctness of this strategy and the need to implement it to the full. The summit agenda tomorrow, however, also tells us that the international system built after the Second World War is now unrecognizable, as you said this morning. With the growth of China and India and the growing influence of non-state actors (meaning business, religious organizations, tribes, criminal networks), with the globalised economy, the transfer of wealth to unprecedented economic power in modern history as to the size, speed and direction that is underway from the West to the East of the world is emerging a multipolar global system that is more unstable than those that preceded it. And I find the American administration's foreign policy polemics exhausting, because it doesn't matter whether we like the United States' foreign policy or not. What really matters is that their financial capacity to pursue it is falling. The U.S. government is also struggling with the ageing population, with a huge debt, health care, pensions. It is on the way to becoming - as has already been said - a domestic insurance company, with a secondary occupation, foreign policy. Within twenty years the service to the public debt - just to give an example - will exceed the entire budget of the Defense and the result will be a leadership with very limited means, even after Obama. And the shortage will bring benefits to the United States and also to other countries, because an era of scarce resources will make the country less inclined to make mistakes, from the expansion of NATO too far east to the disastrous occupation of Iraq, not to mention that the non-conventional oil stations, the shale gas, will lead to a decisive change in the global energy markets. And a centralized policy on reducing national dependence on foreign oil can do, for America and for today's world, what the Soviet Union did in the twentieth century. But less foreign policy in the United States also implies a loss for us, for non-Americans. When the world complains that someone has to do something, the most immediate and disinterested reaction can no longer come from Washington, and other policies of international interest, such as guaranteeing global access to oil, can suffer from it. Obviously there will be limits to the withdrawal from the world of America, but the USA has acted for years like the de facto government of the world and, if they now behave like any other country, the world will have less government. I say this because in this context we have to ask a couple of questions to Europe. A couple of questions we must ask ourselves, because at this stage the arrival of the European superpower is likely to coincide with that of Godot. If the world is going towards the formation of regional blocs that will play the old role of States in the Westfalian system; if continental structures such as America and China, and perhaps India and Brazil, have already reached the critical mass, the question is very simple and even brutal: does Europe want to try or not? Does Europe want to try to face its transition to the rank of regional unity? [[Applause from the PD Group]] Do you want to try to finally achieve a meaningful unity? And would it not be time for Europeans to stop evading the problem of defence policy, as Obama repeated to boredom? And how can we fail to see that the strategic importance of a transatlantic agreement for the liberalisation of trade and investment between the two most advanced economic areas of the planet goes far beyond its economic value? And what should come forward is precisely the awareness, in the absence of a democratic nation strong enough to be a point of reference, of countering the emerging powers of authoritarian capitalism, a new centre capable of exercising an orderly function can only emerge from the global alliance between democracies, cemented by a common market. See that the definition of a common approach is the strongest guarantee for the European Union and the United States to assert their priorities over Moscow, whose capacity for initiative depends more on the divisions of the Western camp than on the use it is able to make of its own resources. And now I say a very quick thing about Syria. In this context something can certainly be done if you take note of what is really happening. It is our interest to eliminate and contain the two threats that are becoming metastases: ISIS, on the one hand, and the tragedy of refugees, on the other, which risks destabilising not only the European Union but also Jordan and Lebanon, in particular. But there is no reason to believe those who say and think simplistically that "miring and pointing fire" can work in Syria better than it did in Iraq and Libya. I read of people who are unable to secure the outskirts of Baltimore or the outskirts of Paris and think they know how to save the center of Aleppo from the sky. This is obviously nonsense. And how can we fail to see that Putin's intervention in defense of Assad, who remains a war criminal, pro-Iranian, alawita, Shiite, will eventually alienate the entire Sunni Muslim world, the vast majority also in Syria. And even if Putin succeeds in defeating ISIS, the only way to keep the situation under control is to replace ISIS with moderate Sunni. But who do you want to be with Russia, if Putin is seen as the defender of those who killed more Sunni than anyone else in the world. Putin will also need American and our help to get away with it and come up with a political solution for Syria. Colleagues, the anchoring to alliance systems with stronger actors able to fill our age-old international security deficit was for Italy the answer to a historical condition of vulnerability. Today, however, the United States no longer has the scale, the strength and even the internal consensus to act as Atlas, who carried the world on his shoulders. The United States is no longer able to guarantee the function of economic locomotive and guarantor of military security. An even more worrying cohesion crisis continues to weigh on the architrave of Italian foreign policy, which is represented by Europe and the European Union. Nowhere is it written that our destiny is of decadence and less regional power, but our future is necessarily linked to that of our European partners. It is precisely from the awareness of this common destiny that the process of European integration must be restarted more decisively, which will force us to do more to relaunch the unification process. The time has ended when we could say safe under the umbrella of NATO and then protest against the missiles in Comiso. Today there is a need for willingness to take risks and invest. Italy has the means, the culture and the potential to do so and has recovered - you are right, President - the solidity in order to do so. We are trying to change Italy and from this effort comes our renewed credibility. That is why we can try to change Europe, its policies (not just governance) and its institutions. In December we will be discussing the so-called report by the five Presidents, but our European institutional system is obviously not the lightning rod to discharge the wrong political decisions and responsibilities. We who voted on the reform of the Constitution yesterday know that the rules can influence the forms and ways of politics. The weakness of its institutions is not alien to Europe's difficulties: it is not the Union that kills children, but its ability to save them is paralyzed by states that refuse to coordinate their choices on the migratory emergency. [[Applause from the PD Group]] It is not the Union that raises the walls and puts barbed wire at the borders, but some states, but the Union cannot prevent it because the powers of intervention which the States as a whole have conferred on it do not allow it. If we really believe in the common future of Europeans, we must not hesitate to call into question the Europe we have to build a better Europe. The Group of the Democratic Party will support the Government's efforts - even more so, Italy's efforts - as they once said, without whether or not. ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u238	Mr President, in the first decades of the Republic, there had never been funding for parties. It was introduced after the oil scandal in 1974, when it was discovered that the government parties were on the payroll of the oilers. The Republican party then demanded public funding. 18.04 (continued ENDRIZZI). The theorem was, if we give the parties the money, we don't steal it. Twenty years later, Tangentopoli showed the Italians that that was a pious illusion, or perhaps a clever lie. In 1993 the Italians therefore expressed themselves clearly and unequivocally in a referendum: via public funding to the parties. The answer, of course, was immediate: change the name from funding to electoral reimbursement. The result: after about twenty more years we in Italy have a level of corruption that is equal to that of the rest of Europe put together. Cases of corruption pave the news every day. They are interspersed only by scandals by distraction of funds or by those for expenditures cheerful with public resources. The Italian champion of this sad behavior, the President-in-Office of the Council, whose political rise we have paid millions of dollars between elephant press offices, representation expenses, to get to the use of the state plane to go and see tennis in America, has just come out. In this smelly mixer we have seen everything: illegal expenses and formally legal but scandalous expenses; green panties, allegorical dinners of politicians with pig masks, African diamonds, villas, cars, even erotic toys put into account. The sense of self-sufficiency of the caste is now pervasive. That's why mayor Marino's receipts are serious. When he declares himself in good faith I believe him, but this shows that he has lost consciousness of the value of his actions. The ethical landslide has involved all the parties and all their members: we will soon have proof of this in the vote on this reference. That is why nobody raised the issue in Rome; none, except the 5 Star Movement, which is born precisely from the conscience of the outraged citizens. But let's come to this bill. Almost twenty years after the law on electoral refunds, under the lighthouse pointed at by scandals, worried by the birth of the 5 Star Movement, the parties have again turned the cards on the table. The prosecutor of the Court of Auditors of Lazio had declared electoral refunds illegitimate and unconstitutional because they were too far from the amounts declared. Since 1994, the parties have pocketed 2.5 billion, against documented expenditure of not even 800 million. It is clear, therefore, that this was still a matter of funding. The solution then was elementary: taking away the jurisdiction of the Court of Auditors - so he learns! - and to set up a guarantee committee which, however, has never had the necessary staff from the Government and therefore today we arrive at a sanatorium. When we talk about parties, the said "made the law," found the deception is no longer true: it is true the said "made the deception," found the law "." For the future, this measure promises a minimum increase in the Commission's organic budget and in return claims retroactive sanatorium: the parties will be able to collect EUR 35 million without having to prove anything. It's the usual game: citizens are offered a ice cream with two balls, chocolate and mud, and if they complain they are told: But you don't like chocolate? It infers, President, with this measure, a massacre on the sense of justice, on the confidence of the citizen on the certainty of the law, as you are doing on the pardon for the capitals held by the mafias abroad. It is a stroke on the principle of equality expressed by Article 3 of the Constitution. Go explain it to Italian companies, artisans, VAT matches: when your accountant is wrong, always answer you, instead the parties when they are wrong dump the blame on the usual Belsito, Fiorito, Trota, Lusi; these take the blame, they show the citizens of the brooms to clean up and the system continues. I know that you will offend these words, as if you were doubting your honesty, but go and say this to the citizens, to the Italian companies on their knees: when they apply to you the field studies, the burden of proof is yours, you have to prove that you did not take that money, but how do you prove something that you have not done? ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u418	Mr President, first I gave a very quick opinion, which I now try to articulate. In my opinion, this amendment does not involve any new legislation, in the sense that Article 331 of the Code of Criminal Procedure states that public officials and public service officers who, in the exercise or because of their duties or service, have news of a criminal offence of office, must report it in writing. Now, because Article 331 of the Code of Criminal Procedure is in force, writing in a law, which we are now approving, that we are applying a law already in force of the same rank, means that practically we are rewriting a law that already exists. This means adding to the law that we are examining an element that brings nothing new. We don't say anything new. Therefore, the Government's opinion must necessarily remain contrary.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u500	Mr Bruni (CoR). Mr President, the Conservative and Reformist Group will vote for the adoption of the draft law that we have just examined. We will do so because we believe that the amendments made to Article 9 of Law No 96 of 2012 are useful and necessary to enable the Commission to ensure that the tasks set out in the above mentioned law are carried out. In fact, the forecast of seven additional units can have the work completed that the Commission has not done so far. We would certainly have liked to improve the bill, with our amendments 1.90 and 1.105, which aimed to submit to the compliance check provided for in Article 9 the statements of the political parties after 2013 and not just 2014; this, in order to increase the transparency effect desired by the law and with the ultimate aim of reconciling the electoral citizen with the political party. I would like to make a few further comments on this very subject. Law No 96 and subsequent Decree-Law No 149 of 2013 are still not implemented, not only as provided for in Article 9, relating to the control of the accounts, but especially as regards the rules concerning the implementation of a true internal democracy in the parties. Articles 2 et seq. of Decree No 149 have no other purpose than to implement the constitutional provision of Article 49. We will remember: "All citizens have the right to join freely in parties in order to contribute democratically to determining national policy". To this end, Article 3 regulates and regulates the statutes of the parties, which must provide for the holding of congress meetings, to be held with precise deadlines. Again, Article 5 focuses on transparency in party life. These forecasts are, to date, mere statements which have no concrete effect. Failure to implement these rules will result in a further increase in the impact on voters and parties. On the other hand, we believe that, together with the issue of funding, it is urgent to resolve the problem of internal democracy of parties. The discussions on the effectiveness of the primary as a method for the selection of the ruling class or those concerning the charismatic parties, in which congresses, assemblies and memberships are an old and abandoned practice, show that the predictions of that decree have not only been implemented so far, but have even been overcome by concrete reality. Yet, in our opinion, the internal democracy of the parties corresponds and guarantees the democracy of the State itself, as a whole. Finally, the issue of funding is one of the issues before us, but very important is how the parties work today and how they try to improve their rules or their way of staying on the political scene. In this sense, there is much talk of the primary and we hope to regulate them in a way that we could define universal or at least accepted by all political forces, so that the rule is always the same for the different sides and in the different places where they are used. I am thinking of the case of other types of consultations, such as those carried out through the web: in both cases, both in the primaries and in the so-called parliamentarians, it would be desirable for the law to establish that information on those who participated in these forms of consultation can be accessed, i.e. that the lists are transparent and accessible to all. It is a way of bringing citizens closer to the lives of political parties, associations or movements. As far as the traditional party is concerned, the last consideration is that it is necessary to guarantee the objectives of which I spoke before and which are indicated in the law, where it is stated that precise congresses must be held, to clarify how to elect the subjects representing those parties and how to indicate the persons who go to be part of the lists. Only in this way will the issue of the financing of the parties not be in itself, but will allow to restore vitality to the formations that contribute to the political life of this country and in this sense our work of today is only part of that work. [[Applause from the CoR Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u519	DEL BARBA (PD). Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the draft law we are about to vote on is very simple, as Senator Bernini reminded us, namely to strengthen the staff of the Commission to guarantee the statutes for the transparency and control of political party statements. It does so with three articles that have been discussed in Parliament since 13 February 2015. It does so by intervening on a concrete situation that sees the 2013 budgets of the parties, certified with the same criteria as the companies that are admitted on the stock exchange, deposited with the Commission with all the supporting pieces in support of each item and until now not examined. It does so at the request of the President of the Guarantee Commission himself, as was mentioned earlier, by intervening to make finally operational what the law already has. At last, they are simply assigned with secondment of five staff units, employees of the Court of Auditors, to be applied to the audit activities and two additional units of staff, dependent on other public administrations, to be applied in the audit activities, all with the appropriate professional competence, to examine the statements of political parties according to the legal dictates. In the absence of such staff, the Commission is not in a position to carry out its mandate in full. Therefore the measure, contrary to what is supported by the 5 Star Movement and by those who are actively opposed to it, is aimed precisely at an effective and full control of regularity with reference to the statements of political parties. In absurd terms, those who are against this measure do not want the Guarantee Commission to function and the party's statements to be verified. [[Applause from the PD Group]] For these simple reasons the Democratic Party will vote in favour of the measure. That, Mr President, should end here the explanation of vote on a measure which is so simple, so obvious, as complementary and consistent with what is already laid down in the law. Instead, it is necessary to participate all together in this great fiction that someone thinks they can assemble and disassemble at will whenever you touch on the issue of financing parties or other subject that is considered sufficiently interesting and manipulatable. A fiction that includes predictable and repetitive scripts, staged also rather worn and stale and above all prescribes the total mystification of reality. [[Applause from the PD Group. The 75-year-old woman is hospitalized in prognosis reserved for the hospital in Livorno and her condition is still serious. Meningitis is scary because it appears suddenly and within a few hours it can get to kill, as unfortunately happened on 30 March last to the nurse of thirty-four years of Vinci. Tuscany, which is the second Region of Italy, after Puglia, as number of cases registered in the last three years (34 cases and six deaths), is doing, fortunately, a widespread campaign for vaccinations. As I did last week, I speak today to return to urge a response from the Higher Institute of Health, which will make us understand better what disease we are dealing with, what is caused by it, what are the reasons why the demonstrations are becoming so frequent in Tuscany. ParlaMint-IT_2015-10-14-LEG17-Senato-sed-523.ana.u560	BAROZZINO (Misto-SEL). Mr President, once again I am trying to talk about work, about the dignity lost in the world of work. I am talking about the fact that the work suits, of very clear color, are stain and cause great discomfort to women, whose dignity in those moments, especially because of their biological diversity, goes to be compromised. However, this is only a part of a problem that arises from the fact that the work breaks have been reduced, from two from twenty minutes each to three from ten minutes, and do not allow workers to go to the bathroom in the utmost tranquility and respect for hygiene rules. I'll explain. Before the breaks were sliding, that is, a worker was replaced by another worker (the problem is very serious, I tell the few intimates that we stayed), so the workers who went to the bathroom were many less. Now the breaks are collective, so in ten minutes you go to the bathroom virtually a whole shift of workers, who therefore have to wait, line up (with all the consequences of the case) in maximum hygiene in ten minutes. If you also add the fact that sometimes the bathrooms are far from the stations (we talk about serious things, they also do hundreds of meters to go to the bathroom), think carefully about what can happen. Fortunately, only a part of that problem has emerged, but the issue is much more serious and goes a lot to the root. Since I always repeat that politics should be concerned with the real facts that happen and as we talk about work, which is the mirror of a country's civilization, I put all this to the attention of the House. I would also like to see all the members of the Committees dealing with work or the Committees of Inquiry into Work take charge of all this, because we are talking about real and concrete things and that, if only politics were interested, they would be easy to resolve. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]
